⏺ This is an analysis task, not implementation — classifying it as a spike: the
  output is an assessment, no code gets written. Your message already defined
  the probe (read the PDF, compare against CLAUDE.md, flag risks), and I've now
  read both, so here are the findings.

  Verdict

  Conceptually, yes — with one missing deliverable and one large risk. The
  Adaptive Repo Tutor squarely satisfies Theme 1 ("a tool for exploring
  codebases"), and the adaptive-learner angle is exactly the "non-obvious"
  quality the assignment asks for. But the plan as written in CLAUDE.md
  describes a 20–40 hour product build against an assignment whose target is 1–2
  hours with a hard cap of 8 — and the assignment explicitly states that
  scoping ability is part of what's evaluated. The concept passes; the plan's
  scope is the threat.

  Requirements mapping

  ┌─────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Assignment requirement  │              CLAUDE.md coverage               │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Theme fit               │ ✅ Theme 1, direct hit ("tool for exploring   │
  │                         │ codebases")                                   │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Deployed,               │ ✅ Railway deployment (Phase 7) — but see     │
  │ browser-usable, no      │ risk #2                                       │
  │ local install           │                                               │
  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Self-contained demo /   │ ✅ §10 makes demo mode a product requirement  │
  │ bundled sample data     │                                               │

  ├─────────────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────┤

  │ "Focus on a single      │ ⚠️ The adaptive loop is that feature — but    │
  │ feature or interaction  │ the plan surrounds it with Postgres, Redis,   │
  │ pattern"                │ ingestion, resume/review, and a test suite    │
  └─────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Risks, in priority order

  1. Scope vs. time budget — the dominant risk. The assignment says "something
  insightful and deeper is more valuable than breadth" and "if you find yourself
  exceeding 8 hours, stop." CLAUDE.md schedules nine phases including a
  Postgres schema with ~9 tables, Redis caching/locking, live GitHub ingestion
  with archive-safety hardening, agentic tool-based repo analysis, and a
  substantial deterministic test suite. Each phase is individually reasonable;
  together they're a multi-day build. Worse, the transcripts you must submit
  will make actual time spent visible — over-scoping isn't just a schedule risk,
  it's a scoring risk, because it reads as failing the explicit scoping test.

  2. Deployment sits at Phase 7 — the critical path is inverted. The assignment
  cannot accept anything requiring local execution. If time runs out at Phase 5,
  you have an impressive local app and no valid submission. The mitigation is
  cheap: deploy the Phase 1 fixture prototype immediately and keep every phase
  deployable. That way there is always a submittable artifact, and each phase
  only improves it.

  3. The video deliverable is absent from the plan. The rationale must be
  delivered in both formats — video and written doc. Phase 9's submission
  checklist covers the written outline but not the recording. Easy to fix,
  painful to discover at 11pm on submission day. Add it to Phase 9 (and reserve
  ~30 minutes for it).

  4. The core differentiator lands late. Visible adaptation — the thing that
  separates this from a "generic AI repo summarizer" — arrives in real form only
  at Phase 5, after persistence (2), ingestion (3), and analysis (4). If budget
  burns on infrastructure, the demo shows a well-persisted, well-ingested app
  with a shallow tutorial loop, which is precisely the mediocre outcome §12
  warns against. Notably, Phase 1's fixture-driven version already demonstrates 
  the entire thesis (adaptation, progress, resume) to an evaluator. Phase 1 +
  deployment is arguably a complete, submittable take-home; live ingestion is
  the stretch goal, not the foundation.

  5. Redis and live-analysis reliability add risk without visible value. For a
  handful of reviewer sessions, Redis caching/locking changes nothing an
  evaluator can see, while adding a service to provision and a failure mode to
  debug. CLAUDE.md itself says "use intentionally, not decoratively" — at this
  scale it's decorative. Similarly, the live "analyze any public repo" path
  depends on GitHub rate limits, repo size, and multi-minute model analysis
  succeeding in front of a reviewer; the fixture demo mitigates this, but every
  failure state in §15 is engineering time spent on the non-core path.

  6. Minor: the model ID. CLAUDE.md pins ANTHROPIC_MODEL=claude-fable-5-1. The
  published model ID is claude-fable-5 — worth verifying against the API before
  it becomes a confusing deploy-time failure. Low risk since it's
  env-configurable.

  One thing the plan gets right relative to the assignment's judgment criterion:
  the phased stop-and-review structure, explicit decision log, and "challenge
  my ideas" instruction mean the transcript will show a human directing the AI
  rather than accepting output — which is exactly what "a fully AI-generated
  project with no judgment will not pass" is screening for. This CLAUDE.md is
  itself strong evidence of that judgment.

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  - Take-home evaluators read scope as signal: an 8-hour cap with "we've seen
  excellent submissions under 2 hours" means the cut list is part of the
  deliverable. CLAUDE.md already contains its own cut list (§11, §20) — the risk
  is that the phase plan doesn't honor it aggressively enough.
  - "Persistence is core" (§14) conflicts subtly with the assignment: resume
  behavior demonstrates the DURABLE property, but localStorage or a single
  SQLite/JSON store demonstrates it to an evaluator just as convincingly as
  Postgres + Redis — the evaluator sees the behavior, never the schema.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  Recommended adjustments before Phase 0 proceeds

  1. Re-sequence: Phase 1 (fixture prototype) → deploy immediately → Phase 5 
  (adaptive loop on fixtures) → then ingestion/persistence as time allows.
  2. Cut Redis entirely; demote Postgres to "if time allows" and start with a
  simpler durable store.
  3. Add the ~5-min video to the Phase 9 checklist with reserved time.
  4. Decide the honest time budget now (e.g., 6 hours of build + 1 hour of
  rationale/video) and let it drive cuts.

  If you want, the next step per CLAUDE.md is the Phase 0 response (questions
  A–K) — I'd fold these scope adjustments into that proposal. Otherwise, tell me
  which of these risks you want to accept versus fix, and I'll update CLAUDE.md
  accordingly.

     Re-scope the plan, and include at the end a script I can help use to record
     the self-recorded video that explain the rational. I remember learning 
     from one of the best teacher I had that a great way to learn is to read a

     come up with the answers. I also find myself very often instructing the 
     chat to feed me small chunk of information when learning a new subject and 
     test me before moving on to the next set of concepts. I found it to be a 
     very effective way of learning. finally, analogies that are easy to 
     remember and clear contrast with altearnative repos or technologies are 
     very helpful in the learning process. A great teacher also tries to 
     undestand constantly where is the student understanding and dynamically 
     adjust the curriculum, methods, reviews and introduction of new concepts to
     deepen the student understanding. This project tries to incorporate all 
     those ideas and apply them when learning a new large code base.

  Proposed re-scope of CLAUDE.md

  1. Re-sequenced phases, with an "always submittable" invariant. Every phase
  after the first ends with a working deployed app:

  ┌─────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┬──────┐
  │ New │                            What                            │ Was  │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ 0   │ Think before coding (unchanged)                            │ 0    │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ 1   │ Fixture UX prototype — shell, sidebar, demo repo, adaptive │ 1    │
  │     │  loop on fixtures, resume choices                          │      │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ 2   │ Deploy immediately — Railway web service only, no DB, no   │ was  │
  │     │ Redis                                                      │ 7    │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │     │ Real adaptive loop via Anthropic API on the bundled        │      │
  │ 3   │ demo-repo fixture — lesson generation, ≤3-question tests,  │ was  │
  │     │ grading, learner-state update, visible adaptation,         │ 5    │
  │     │ coverage %                                                 │      │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ 4   │ Persistence — minimal Postgres, durable browser identity,  │ was  │
  │     │ sidebar history, resume                                    │ 2    │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │     │ Live GitHub ingestion + repo analysis (stretch goal) —     │ was  │
  │ 5   │ seed-files approach first; agentic read tools only if time │ 3+4  │
  │     │  remains                                                   │      │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ 6   │ Polish + failure states                                    │ was  │
  │     │                                                            │ 8    │
  ├─────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼──────┤
  │ 7   │ Submission package — final review, written rationale,      │ was  │
  │     │ video script + recording, transcripts, honest time note    │ 9    │
  └─────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴──────┘

  The old Redis phase is deleted; Redis moves to §20 "Future Extensions". The
  rationale for the re-ordering: Phase 3 is the thesis — if time dies after it,
  you still submit a deployed, adaptive, demo-mode product, which is a complete
  take-home. Persistence and live ingestion each upgrade the submission rather
  than gate it.

  2. Infrastructure cuts. Redis removed from the stack, env vars, and deployment
  sections. Postgres deferred until after the adaptive loop is proven (with a
  note that the sidebar/resume in Phases 1–3 runs on fixtures/localStorage until
  then).

  3. Video deliverable added. New file in §16: docs/VIDEO_SCRIPT.md — a
  shot-by-shot ~5-minute script I'll help you write in Phase 7. Structure: open
  with your learning philosophy as the hook (~45s), live demo following the
  evaluator journey (~2.5 min), key decisions and tradeoffs (~1 min), "with more
  time" + time spent (~45s). Recording time reserved in the Phase 7 checklist.

  4. Pedagogy narrative woven in. Your four principles — small chunks with
  self-questioning, test-before-advance, memorable analogies with contrast to
  alternatives, continuous assessment driving dynamic adjustment — get added to
  §1 as the product's origin story and to §12's "what makes this non-obvious"
  list, and the lesson rules in §3 get traced back to them explicitly. This
  gives the rationale and transcript a clear authored vision, which is precisely
  what the "judgment" criterion screens for.

  5. Explicit time budget. A line in the working-style section: ~6 hours build,
  ~1 hour rationale + video, actual time reported honestly. (Tell me if you want
  a different split.)

  6. Minor fix. Note to verify the ANTHROPIC_MODEL default ID (claude-fable-5-1
  may not be a valid API ID; it stays env-configurable regardless).

  Only CLAUDE.md is touched — no code, no doc files created yet (those come in
  their phases). Shall I apply this amendment?

          + is still a complete, deployed, demo-mode submission. Persiste
          +nce and live ingestion UPGRADE the submission; they do not gat
          +e it.                                                         
      871 +

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  - The structural change with the biggest payoff is moving deployment from 7th
  to 2nd. It converts "will it deploy?" from an end-of-project cliff into a
  solved problem, and turns every subsequent phase into an increment on a live,
  submittable artifact — the same reason CI systems deploy walking skeletons
  before features.
  - Keeping the Playwright suite deliberately tiny (3–6 journey tests) is a best
  practice, not a compromise: e2e tests are the most expensive to maintain, so
  they should guard the one path that must never break — here, the evaluator's
  demo journey.

❯ proceed with Phase 0                                                          

⏺ Beginning Phase 0 per the working-style ritual:

  - Objective: think before coding — challenge the concept, lock the evaluator
  journey, define the data models and progress semantics, draft docs/PRODUCT.md
  + docs/DECISIONS.md, then stop.
  - Smallest useful implementation: the A–K answers below plus the two doc
  files. No application code.
  - Key tradeoffs in play: guaranteed demo path vs. live-repo generality;
  explicit state models vs. chat-transcript simplicity; honest-but-coarse
  progress math vs. false precision.
  - What I propose to change: create docs/PRODUCT.md and docs/DECISIONS.md only.

  ---
  A. The product in one paragraph

  An adaptive tutor that teaches you an unfamiliar public GitHub repository the
  way a great teacher would: it builds a structured model of the repository
  (what must be understood) and a continuously updated model of you (what you
  currently understand), then runs a calm loop — one small concept grounded in
  real code, a test of at most three questions, honest grading, and a next step
  chosen from both models. It explains with memorable analogies and contrast
  against alternative tools, makes every adaptation visible ("you've got the
  pipeline; ordering is still shaky, so we'll look at the stack next"), and
  persists the journey so you can leave, return from the sidebar, and continue
  or actively review — with progress meaning goal-scoped curriculum coverage,
  never a fake intelligence score.

  B. Strongest non-obvious insight

  The curriculum does not exist ahead of time — it is planned one step at a time
  from assessment evidence. Every explainer tool (and every "explain this repo"
  chat) optimizes the artifact. This product optimizes the gap between two 
  state models: RepositoryModel (what must be understood) minus LearnerState
  (what is understood), and the next lesson is the policy that closes that gap.
  The corollary that makes it work: assessment is not a quiz feature, it's the 
  sensor. Without testing, "adaptive" is guessing; with it, adaptation becomes
  auditable — you can point at the answer that caused the path to change. This
  is also precisely what an ordinary LLM chat can't sustain: transcripts bloat
  and drift, while a compact, schema-validated learner state stays durable,
  cheap to prompt with, and honest.

  Challenges to the concept (before committing)

  Three honest pushbacks, and where I landed:

  1. "Is adaptivity even demonstrable in a 3-minute evaluation?" Genuine risk —
  believable adaptation normally needs many steps. Resolution: engineer one 
  crisp branch into the demo (right answer → deepen; wrong answer → visibly
  remediate with a different concept 2). One well-designed fork proves the
  mechanism; ten shallow steps wouldn't.
  2. "Are two state models overkill for a take-home?" No — but only because we
  keep them small. The two-model framing is the thesis; cutting it leaves a
  summarizer. What we cut instead is model richness: fixture curriculum covers
  levels 1–4, not all 7.
  3. "Free-form grading is the flakiest part — why include it?" Because grading
  semantically is where Claude earns its place. Mitigation: multiple choice
  stays deterministic (application code), free-form grading returns a
  schema-validated assessment object, and the demo script leans on MC for
  reliability while showcasing one free-form grade.

  C. Three biggest ways this becomes a mediocre take-home

  1. Fake or invisible adaptation — the next lesson doesn't actually depend on
  the answer, or the dependence isn't legible. This is the "AI repo summarizer
  with quizzes bolted on" failure. Avoidance: the adaptation message must cite
  the specific evidence ("you said X, which tells me Y"), and the demo branch
  must produce different concept 2s for different answers. Generic questions
  ("what does this project do?") ungrounded in code are the same failure in
  question form.
  2. Infrastructure burn — spending the budget on ingestion, schema, and deploy
  plumbing, leaving a shallow loop. Avoidance: already re-scoped — loop
  (Phase 3) before persistence (4) and ingestion (5, stretch).
  3. A fragile first minute — evaluator hits a multi-minute analysis, an API
  error, or an empty state before seeing the idea. Avoidance: the bundled demo
  path is precomputed and guaranteed; live analysis is a stretch upgrade;
  Playwright guards the demo journey in CI.

  How we structurally avoid "generic summarizer": a summarizer has one state
  model (the repo), runs once, and outputs prose. We have a second state model
  (the learner), run a loop, and output decisions — teach/test/remediate — with
  prose as the medium. Every screen should make the learner model's existence
  felt.

  D. Smallest end-to-end evaluator journey that proves the idea

  Under 3 minutes, zero reviewer-supplied data:

  1. Open the deployed URL → click "Try the demo" (no input needed).
  2. Concept 1 appears: short explanation with an analogy, a real code excerpt
  with path + line range, 2 questions beneath.
  3. Answer (get one wrong, or right — both are handled) → see grading, a
  visible adaptation message naming what the answer revealed, and progress move
  (0% → ~9%).
  4. Concept 2 arrives and demonstrably depends on the answer given.
  5. Click away to home → the journey sits in the sidebar with its percentage →
  click it → resume screen: Continue / Review last lesson / Review what I've 
  learned → pick "Review last lesson" → answer 1–2 questions → see
  mastery/confidence acknowledged.

  That single pass exhibits all three product properties: adaptive (step 4),
  durable (step 5), progressive (the coverage % and goal framing).

  E. Infrastructure NOT to build until later, and why

  Already codified in the re-scoped CLAUDE.md; restating the judgment:

  ┌─────────────────────┬────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┐
  │      Deferred       │    To when     │               Why                │
  ├─────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Redis               │ Never (MVP)    │ Invisible to the evaluator; adds │
  │                     │                │  a service + failure mode        │
  ├─────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │                     │                │ The loop must prove itself       │
  │ Postgres            │ Phase 4        │ first; lightweight store demos   │
  │                     │                │ resume fine                      │
  ├─────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Agentic analysis    │ Phase 5, time  │ Seed-file analysis gives 80% of  │
  │ tools (read_file    │ permitting     │ the RepositoryModel quality      │
  │ etc.)               │                │                                  │
  ├─────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Live GitHub         │ Phase 5        │ Least reliable component; demo   │
  │ ingestion           │ (stretch)      │ path must not depend on it       │
  ├─────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Vector DB /         │ Never (MVP)    │ Tree + keyword search suffices   │
  │ embeddings          │                │ at this scale                    │
  ├─────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │                     │                │ Browser identity demonstrates    │
  │ Auth                │ Never (MVP)    │ durability; accounts prove       │
  │                     │                │ nothing extra                    │
  ├─────────────────────┼────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Workers / job       │ Never (MVP)    │ No demonstrated need             │
  │ queues              │                │                                  │
  └─────────────────────┴────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┘

  The shared reason: none of these change what the evaluator sees in the
  guaranteed path, and the assignment explicitly scores scoping judgment.

  F. Data models

  ★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────
  - The design rule underneath all three models: LearnerState is a current 
  snapshot, not an event log. History lives in TutorialSteps (append-only
  evidence); the snapshot is what gets sent to the model each turn. This keeps
  prompts small and makes "resume" trivial — load one row, not a replay.
  - Facts and inferences are separate arrays by design, so the UI can never
  accidentally present a guessed maintainer intent as ground truth — the honesty
  constraint is enforced by the schema, not by prompt discipline.
  ─────────────────────────────────────────────────

  RepositoryModel — built once per (repoUrl, commitSha), shared across learners:

  {
    id, repoUrl, owner, name, defaultBranch, commitSha,
    description, languages: string[],
    summary: { readme, setup, usage },
    architecture: {
      components: [{ id, name, purpose, paths: string[] }],
      flows: [{ id, name, steps: string[] }]
    },
    evidence: [{ id, path, startLine, endLine, excerpt, note }],
    facts: [{ claim, evidenceIds: string[] }],          // observed
    inferences: [{ claim, rationale, evidenceIds: string[] }], // marked as 
  inference
    concepts: [{
      id, title, level: 1..7, summary,
      prerequisites: conceptId[],
      weight: number,                  // importance for coverage
      goals: Goal[],                   // which goals include this concept
      evidenceIds: string[]
    }]
  }

  LearningJourney — one user × one repo × one goal; the durable container:

  {
    id, userId /* browser identity */, repositoryModelId, repoDisplayName,
    goal: 'understand' | 'use' | 'architecture' | 'contribute',
    questionStyle: 'mc' | 'free' | 'mixed',
    createdAt, lastActiveAt,
    steps: TutorialStep[],      // append-only history
    reviews: ReviewAttempt[]
  }
  // TutorialStep: { id, conceptId, explanation, excerptRef, questions[],
  //                 answers[], assessment, adaptationMessage, nextDecision, 
  createdAt }

  LearnerState — compact current snapshot per journey:

  {
    journeyId,
    conceptStatus: { [conceptId]: 'untaught' | 'taught_untested' | 'understood'
                                | 'partial' | 'misconception' },
    mastery: { [conceptId]: { score: 0..1, confidence: 0..1, lastTestedAt } },
    misconceptions: [{ conceptId, description, detectedAt, resolvedAt? }],
    examinedCodeAreas: string[],
    recentEvidence: AssessmentSummary[],   // last ~3, evidence not 
  chain-of-thought
    recommendedNext: { action:
  'advance'|'reinforce'|'remediate'|'review_prereq',
                       conceptId, reason },
    coverage: { percent, inScopeConceptIds, creditedConceptIds }
  }

  G. Curriculum coverage percentage — exact definition and limitations

  Definition. For a journey with goal g:
  - In-scope set S = all concepts whose goals include g, plus any prerequisites
  the adaptive planner pulls in.
  - Each concept c has weight w(c) from the RepositoryModel.
  - Credit: 0 if untaught · 0.5 if taught but never assessed · 1.0 if taught and
  assessed at least once — regardless of the mastery score.
  - Coverage % = round(100 × Σ w(c)·credit(c) / Σ w(c)), over S.

  Behavior rules. Reviews never mint coverage from thin air — but if review
  tests a concept that was taught-untested, credit legitimately rises 0.5 → 1.0.
  Mastery scores move independently and drive adaptation, never the percentage.
  If remediation adds a prerequisite, S grows and the percentage may dip
  slightly; the UI says why ("added a prerequisite to your path") rather than
  hiding it.

  Limitations, stated plainly: weights are model-inferred, not ground truth; the
  0.5/1.0 quantization is deliberately coarse (understandable beats precise);
  the number measures traversal, not understanding — that separation is a
  feature, but it means 100% coverage with weak mastery is possible; the
  denominator is not perfectly stable; and percentages are incomparable across
  goals or repos.

  H. Exact Phase 1 fixture/demo interaction

  The three interaction moments the whole product hangs on: (1) the visible 
  adaptation message, (2) the resume ritual, (3) code-as-assessment. The fixture
  is scripted to hit all three.

  Demo repo: expressjs/express. Famous enough that an evaluator has instant
  context, small enough to model honestly, and its middleware pipeline is ideal
  analogy/contrast material (vs. Koa, Fastify).

  Fixture setup. Sidebar shows one pre-existing journey — fastapi/fastapi · 62% 
  · 2d ago — proving history at a glance. The demo button starts a fresh
  expressjs/express journey.

  The script:
  1. Home: URL input, test-style selector, "Learn this repo" primary, subtle
  "Try the demo".
  2. Demo click → ~2s of staged analysis states ("Reading project structure…",
  "Building your learning path…") → micro-onboarding: goal (default Understand 
  the architecture), style (mixed).
  3. Concept 1 (Level 1): "Express is two ideas: a router and a middleware
  pipeline wrapped around Node's http server." Analogy: airport security — each
  middleware is a checkpoint that inspects, modifies, or turns away the request;
  the route handler is the gate. Excerpt: lib/express.js createApplication (~15
  lines, path + line range shown). Two questions: one MC on what app.use(fn)
  does, one short free-form on middleware ordering.
  4. The branch (fixture contains both):
    - Correct → adaptation: "You've got the pipeline model. Your goal is
  architecture, so next: how routing layers on top of it." → Concept 2a: the
  Router.
    - Wrong on ordering → adaptation: "Your answer suggests middleware order is
  still fuzzy — order is the heart of the pipeline. Before going deeper, let's
  look at how the stack is actually stored." → Concept 2b: remediation via
  lib/router/index.js stack excerpt.
    - Either way progress moves 0% → 9% (concept 1 taught + assessed), and the
  difference between 2a and 2b is the demo's money shot.
  5. Leave and return: navigate home; sidebar shows expressjs/express · 9% · 
  just now; click it → resume screen: "Welcome back — 9% covered toward
  Understand the architecture" with Continue / Review last lesson / Review what 
  I've learned.
  6. Each resume option works in fixture form: Continue → concept 2; Review last
  lesson → 2 questions on concept 1 → graded, confidence note; Review what I've
  learned → short mixed review with a one-line explanation of why these
  questions were chosen (weak/stale-weighted).

  All deterministic, no API, no DB.

  Understory (the layer beneath the canopy — learning beneath the code), Onramp
  (ramp-up to contribution), RepoTutor (does what it says). I'd default to

⏺ Write(src/lib/time.ts)
  ⎿  Wrote 10 lines to src/lib/time.ts
       1 export function timeAgo(now: number, then: number): string {
       2   const seconds = Math.max(0, Math.floor((now - then) / 1000));
       3   if (seconds < 90) return "just now";
       4   const minutes = Math.floor(seconds / 60);
       5   if (minutes < 60) return `${minutes}m ago`;
       6   const hours = Math.floor(minutes / 60);
       7   if (hours < 36) return `${hours}h ago`;
       8   const days = Math.floor(hours / 24);
       9   return `${days}d ago`;
      10 }

       13 +function validateRepoUrl(value: string): string | null {

       16 +  let url: URL;

          +/${trimmed}`);

          +he moment.";                                                  
       24 +  }

⏺ Phase 1 complete — summary, checklist, STOP

  What was built (all fixture-driven; no API, no database):

  - The shell: dark-pine sidebar with recent journeys showing computed coverage
  percentages and recency, active-journey indicator, calm paper-cream reading
  canvas. Design direction: "editorial understory" — Fraunces for reading,
  Schibsted Grotesk for UI, IBM Plex Mono for code.
  - Home: one-line pitch, GitHub URL input with honest validation (non-GitHub
  URLs get the "not supported yet" message), question-style control, and the
  subtle demo entry.
  - The demo loop on expressjs/express: staged analysis → 30-second onboarding →
  lesson (analogy-driven prose + code excerpt with real line numbers) →
  ≤3-question quiz → graded feedback → the adaptation margin-note → progress
  moving. The branch is real: answer the middleware-ordering question wrong and
  the curriculum reroutes through a remediation lesson on the layer stack, with
  the coverage denominator visibly growing ("path grew: +Where order lives").
  Answer right and you advance to the router instead.
  - Durable resume: leave, reload the browser entirely, return from the sidebar
  → "Welcome back · 10% covered · Continue (shoring up a gap first) / Quick
  review / Review what I've learned", plus explicit demo restart.
  - Active review: the seeded fastapi journey (64%) samples its two
  weakest/stalest concepts for broad review; a wrong answer updates mastery and
  redirects the path — verified live.
  - Engineering: 21 unit tests on coverage math, grading, and state transitions;
  typecheck, lint, and production build all clean; the whole flow (both
  branches, resume, review) verified in a real browser via Playwright.

  Two bugs found and fixed during verification: the scaffold briefly clobbered
  CLAUDE.md (restored from the Phase 0 commit — committing early paid off), and
  seeded journeys showed onboarding instead of resume because history lives in
  learner state, not step records.

  Your manual test checklist (~4 minutes — run npm run dev, open
  localhost:3000):
  1. Home: paste gitlab.com/foo/bar → non-GitHub message appears; sidebar shows
  fastapi/fastapi at 64%.
  2. Click Try the demo → analysis stages → onboarding → lesson 1 reads well
  (judge the prose quality — it's the product).
  3. Answer Q2 wrong ("Express analyzes…") → check the adaptation note names the
  misconception, coverage hits 10%, header shows "path grew", Continue targets
  Where order lives.
  4. Click the Understory wordmark to leave, then the express journey in the
  sidebar → resume screen with three options; try Restart this demo and this
  time answer everything right → different concept 2 (The router). That A/B
  difference is the thesis.
  5. Open fastapi/fastapi → Review what I've learned → get one wrong → the path
  redirects to Pydantic.
  6. Keyboard-only pass: tab through home and a quiz — focus rings should be
  visible everywhere.

  Remaining risks: the journey-screen.tsx component is large (~700 lines — fine
  for a prototype, worth splitting when Phase 3 touches it); free-form grading
  is a keyword heuristic (by design, until Claude arrives); the goal selector is
  locked to "architecture" until live analysis exists.

  STOPPING per the ritual. Next up when you give the word: Phase 2 — 
  walking-skeleton deployment + CI (GitHub repo, Actions pipeline, Railway,
  health endpoint).

⏺ Phase 3 ritual — the thesis phase:

  - Objective: replace the scripted decide() functions with real Claude: lesson
  generation grounded in repo evidence, semantic grading of free-form answers,
  learner-state updates, and next-step decisions — all server-side, all
  schema-validated, running against the bundled demo repo.
  - Smallest useful implementation: three API routes (/api/lesson, /api/assess,
  /api/review) sharing one Anthropic client; zod validation on every model
  output; deterministic guardrails app-side (MC grading, status transitions,
  next-concept validation with fallback); client wiring with calm
  loading/error/retry states. Concept summaries + small evidence excerpts added
  to fixtures so Claude can ground lessons for every concept, not just the three
  scripted ones.
  - Key tradeoffs:
    - Fixture fallback stays: when ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is absent or a call fails,
  routes fall back to the Phase 1 scripted engine. This keeps CI deterministic
  (runners have no key), keeps the demo alive through API outages, and gives a
  clean retry path. The UI will show a subtle indicator in fixture mode —
  honest, not hidden.
    - One model call for grade + next-step (not two) — halves latency and the
  decision sees the grading evidence directly.
    - No streaming yet — structured JSON validation beats perceived latency for
  ≤3-question steps; good loading copy instead. Revisit in polish.
  - What changes: src/lib/server/ (client, prompts, schemas), three routes,
  fixture enrichment, journey-screen wiring, mocked-boundary route tests.

      148 +// Generic outcome for concepts without a scripted decide() — 
          +keeps the                                                     
      149 +// fixture fallback working at any curriculum depth.          
      150 +export function genericOutcome(                               
      151 +  journey: LearningJourney,                                   
      152 +  conceptId: string,                                          
      153 +  correct: Record<string, boolean>,                           
      154 +): StepOutcome {                                              
      155 +  const wrong = Object.values(correct).filter((v) => !v).lengt
          +h;                                                            
      156 +  const allRight = wrong === 0;                               
      157 +  const nextId = nextUntaughtConcept({                        
      158 +    ...journey,                                               
      159 +    learner: {                                                
      160 +      ...journey.learner,                                     
      161 +      conceptStatus: {                                        
      162 +        ...journey.learner.conceptStatus,                     
      163 +        [conceptId]: "understood",                            
      164 +      },                                                      
      165 +    },                                                        
      166 +  });                                                         
      167 +  return {                                                    
      168 +    conceptStatus: allRight ? "understood" : "partial",       
      169 +    nextConceptId: nextId,                                    
      170 +    adaptationMessage: allRight                               
      171 +      ? "That landed. Your path continues to the next concept 
          +in scope."                                                    
      172 +      : "Part of that is still soft — we'll keep it in view as
          + we continue.",                                               
      173 +    correct,                                                  
      174 +    assessment: {                                             
      175 +      mastery: allRight ? 0.85 : 0.55,                        
      176 +      conceptsDemonstrated: [],                               
      177 +      misconceptions: [],                                     
      178 +      missingPoints: [],                                      
      179 +      feedback: allRight                                      
      180 +        ? "All answers correct."                              
      181 +        : "Some answers missed — worth a second look before mo
          +ving deep.",                                                  
      182 +      recommendedAction: allRight ? "advance" : "reinforce",  
      183 +      confidence: 0.6,                                        
      184 +    },                                                        
      185 +  };                                                          
      186 +}                                                             
      187 +                                                              
      188 +// Fixture-path submission: grade deterministically, run the s
          +cripted (or                                                   
      189 +// generic) decision, apply. The server route mirrors this sha
          +pe with a                                                     
      190 +// Claude decision instead. Grades only the questions actually
          + asked.                                                       
      191 +export function submitLesson(                                 
      192 +  journey: LearningJourney,                                   
      193 +  lesson: Lesson,                                             
      194 +  asked: Question[],                                          
      195 +  answers: Record<string, string>,                            
      196 +  now: number,                                                
      197 +): SubmitResult {                                             
      198 +  const content = getRepoContent(journey.repoId);             
      199 +  const correct: Record<string, boolean> = {};                
      200 +  for (const q of asked) {                                    
      201 +    correct[q.id] = gradeAnswer(q, answers[q.id] ?? "");      
      202 +  }                                                           
      203 +                                                              
      204 +  const decide = content.decide[lesson.conceptId];            
      205 +  const outcome: StepOutcome = decide                         
      206 +    ? { ...decide(correct), correct }                         
      207 +    : genericOutcome(journey, lesson.conceptId, correct);     
      208 +                                                              
      209 +  return applyStepOutcome(journey, lesson.conceptId, answers, 
          +outcome, now);                                                
      210 +}

  ⎿  Added 63 lines
      120  
      121  ---
      122  
      123 +## 009 — Fixture fallback behind the same API (2026-09-03)    
      124 +                                                              
      125 +**Decision:** The three loop routes serve Claude-generated con
          +tent when                                                     
      126 +`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is configured and fall back to the Phase 1
          + scripted engine                                              
      127 +(same response shape, `source: "fixture"`) when it isn't or wh
          +en a call fails.                                              
      128 +                                                              
      129 +**Why:** CI stays deterministic without a key; the demo surviv
          +es API outages;                                               
      130 +the UI needs one code path. The fallback is honest — the UI ta
          +gs scripted                                                   
      131 +content.                                                      
      132 +                                                              
      133 +**Alternatives:** Model-only with hard errors; separate fixtur
          +e UI path.                                                    
      134 +                                                              
      135 +**Tradeoff:** Fixture depth is limited to scripted concepts; a
          +n unscripted                                                  
      136 +concept without a key returns a clear 503.                    
      137 +                                                              
      138 +---                                                           
      139 +                                                              
      140 +## 010 — One model call for grading + next-step decision (2026
          +-09-03)                                                       
      141 +                                                              
      142 +**Decision:** `/api/assess` grades free-form answers, produces
          + the assessment,                                              
      143 +and decides the next concept in a single structured call.     
      144 +                                                              
      145 +**Why:** Halves the perceived latency of the loop's slowest mo
          +ment, and the                                                 
      146 +decision sees the grading evidence directly instead of a summa
          +ry of it.                                                     
      147 +                                                              
      148 +**Alternatives:** Separate grade and plan calls.              
      149 +                                                              
      150 +**Tradeoff:** A schema failure loses both outputs at once — mi
          +tigated by the                                                
      151 +deterministic fallback.                                       
      152 +                                                              
      153 +---                                                           
      154 +                                                              
      155 +## 011 — Reviews are multiple-choice only (2026-09-03)        
      156 +                                                              
      157 +**Decision:** Review questions (quick and broad) are MC-only; 
          +grading is                                                    
      158 +deterministic application code.                               
      159 +                                                              
      160 +**Why:** Reviews are quick retention checks, not essays; deter
          +ministic grading                                              
      161 +keeps them instant and keeps `submitReview` client-side and te
          +stable.                                                       
      162 +                                                              
      163 +**Alternatives:** Free-form reviews graded by the model.      
      164 +                                                              
      165 +**Tradeoff:** Less expressive retention evidence; acceptable —
          + lessons carry                                                
      166 +the free-form load.                                           
      167 +                                                              
      168 +---                                                           
      169 +                                                              
      170 +## 012 — Model default corrected to claude-fable-5 (2026-09-03
          +)                                                             
      171 +                                                              
      172 +**Decision:** `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` defaults to `claude-fable-5`; 
          +CLAUDE.md's                                                   
      173 +`claude-fable-5-1` does not exist in the API catalog.         
      174 +                                                              
      175 +**Why:** The instruction's intent was "newest frontier model";
          + the env var                                                  
      176 +keeps it swappable without code changes. Effort defaults to `m
          +edium` for                                                    
      177 +interactive latency.                                          
      178 +                                                              
      179 +**Alternatives:** `claude-opus-4-8` (cheaper, faster).        
      180 +                                                              
      181 +**Tradeoff:** Fable costs ~2× Opus per token and thinks longer
          +; per-lesson                                                  
      182 +cost is still cents at demo scale.                            
      183 +                                                              
      184 +---

⏺ Phase 4 ritual:

  - Objective: durable persistence — journeys, learner state, steps, and reviews
  in Railway Postgres behind a per-browser cookie identity, with the sidebar
  and resume served from the database; localStorage stays as the offline
  fallback so the demo survives a database outage (explicit phase requirement).
  - Smallest useful implementation: 4 tables (users, journeys, steps, reviews),
  one identity helper (httpOnly cookie), three routes (GET /api/journeys,
  PUT/DELETE /api/journeys/[id]), and a store rework that loads server-first and
  dual-writes. Steps/reviews append-only; learner state as jsonb.
  - Key tradeoffs:
    - jsonb payloads, keyed columns only for what we query (user, repo,
  last-active). You flagged the data structures may still change — jsonb absorbs
  that churn without migrations; we normalize later if querying demands it.
    - Drizzle ORM + SQL migrations run at container boot (migrate && next 
  start): Railway's private network means the DB isn't reachable from CI, so
  boot-time migration is the honest place; drizzle is light enough to not fight
  us.
    - No DATABASE_URL → clean degradation to localStorage — same pattern as the
  missing API key: CI/e2e stay deterministic, local dev needs no DB.
    - The repositories/snapshots/analyses tables from §8 wait for Phase 5 —
  fixture RepositoryModels are code, not data.
  - What I'll change: src/lib/server/db/*, identity.ts, journeys.ts, two route
  files, store.ts rework, migration script + start command, tests for the
  serialization/diff logic.

  ⎿  Added 21 lines
      183  
      184  ---
      185  
      186 +## 013 — Persistence: jsonb payloads + dual-write with local f
          +allback (2026-09-03)                                          
      187 +                                                              
      188 +**Decision:** Four tables (users, journeys, steps, reviews). K
          +eyed columns only                                             
      189 +for what we query (user, repo, recency); learner state and ste
          +p/review records                                              
      190 +are jsonb. The client loads server-first and keeps localStorag
          +e as a                                                        
      191 +write-through cache and fallback; a failed sync degrades to lo
          +cal mode.                                                     
      192 +                                                              
      193 +**Why:** The data structures are still expected to change (exp
          +licit product                                                 
      194 +constraint) — jsonb absorbs churn without migrations. The fall
          +back satisfies                                                
      195 +"the demo keeps working even if external services fail" and ke
          +eps CI                                                        
      196 +deterministic with no database.                               
      197 +                                                              
      198 +**Alternatives:** Fully normalized schema; Prisma; server-only
          + state with hard                                              
      199 +errors.                                                       
      200 +                                                              
      201 +**Tradeoff:** Can't query inside learner state with SQL (not n
          +eeded yet);                                                   
      202 +local-mode writes are not synced back when the server returns 
          +(acceptable —                                                 
      203 +sessions are short; the server copy wins on next load).       
      204 +

❯ while we wait for CD to finish and my manual tests - can we in parallel start 
  working on Phase 5?                                                           

⏺ Yes — good use of the wait. Phase 5 ritual first:

  - Objective (stretch): paste any public GitHub URL → deterministic ingestion
  (metadata, commit SHA, file tree, seed files — all via the GitHub REST API, no
  archive extraction, no code execution) → one structured Claude analysis
  producing a real RepositoryModel with concepts, weights, goal applicability,
  and code evidence → persisted by repo+commit → taught by the exact same
  adaptive loop.
  - Smallest useful implementation: seed-files analysis only (no agentic read
  tools yet — CLAUDE.md says add those only if time clearly remains); REST
  contents API instead of tarballs (sidesteps path-traversal and size traps
  entirely); analysis stored in an analyses table keyed (repoId, commitSha); the
  journey carries a copy of its model (new nullable jsonb column) so the
  client's coverage math and sidebar work unchanged — and live journeys survive
  even in localStorage-fallback mode.
  - Tradeoffs: model duplicated per journey (bytes are trivial at this scale,
  and it buys offline resilience); live repos have no fixture fallback — a
  failed model call surfaces a retry, which is honest for a stretch path;
  unauthenticated GitHub allows ~60 req/hr (an analysis costs ~15) — optional
  GITHUB_TOKEN raises it.
  - Parallel-work discipline: everything goes on a branch
  (phase5-live-ingestion) so main stays exactly what you're manually testing —
  the always-submittable invariant holds, and CI validates the branch via PR
  without deploying. We merge only after your Phase 4 sign-off.

  Starting with the deterministic ingestion layer:

❯ just to confirm, we will share with Claude API in the request the repo to be learned and analyzed correct? the site is not going to send the repo content or anything of that sort. 

⏺ Good moment to be precise about this, because it's the opposite of what you're
  describing — we do send repository content to the Claude API, deliberately
  and boundedly. Here's exactly what crosses the wire:

  What our server sends to Anthropic per analysis request:
  1. Repo metadata (name, description, primary language, commit SHA),
  2. the filtered file-path listing (paths and sizes only — up to 1,500 entries,
  with vendored dirs/binaries/lockfiles already stripped),
  3. the actual contents of up to 12 "seed files" — README, manifests
  (package.json etc.), and obvious entrypoints — capped at 48KB per file and
  ~280KB total.

  Later, each lesson request re-sends only the small stored code excerpt (5–20
  lines) relevant to that concept — never the repo again.

  Why it must work this way: the Claude API has no ability to browse GitHub in
  our design — no web tools, no repo access. The model can only teach from what
  we hand it. "Send just the URL" would require giving Claude a fetch tool
  (that's the optional Stage-B agentic-tools extension from CLAUDE.md §5,
  explicitly deferred), and even then Anthropic's servers would fetch the
  content — it still reaches the API either way.

  The boundaries that make this OK:
  - MVP is public GitHub repos only — nothing sent that isn't already
  world-readable.
  - Everything is selected by deterministic server code with hard caps; the
  browser never talks to Anthropic directly; your API key never leaves the
  server.
  - Anthropic API data handling applies: standard 30-day retention (which Fable
  5 requires), not used for model training by default.

  This matches CLAUDE.md §5's design ("give Claude the tree + high-value seed
  files") — but if you'd rather constrain it further (e.g., README+manifests
  only, no source files), say so and I'll tighten the seed selector. Otherwise
  I'll continue building the analyze route on this design.

❯ I think we should use the agentic capability. share the repo url only  (after 
  confirming it is public), ask it to learn it giving the relevant purpose of

  relevant data structure and context. At least the first time the user

⏺ Two things landed while I was working — the Phase 4 verification completed
  (all green), and your design direction for Phase 5. Taking them in order:

  Phase 4 persistence verified in production ✅: a fresh "browser" (cookie jar
  A) got its own seeded journey; a PUT with one completed step survived a
  re-fetch with learner state intact; a second "browser" (jar B) saw only its
  own fresh seed — full isolation. Postgres migrations ran at boot. Phase 4 is
  done pending your manual sign-off.

  On your agentic design — I agree, and it's within budget. Assessment before I
  build:

  - "Share the URL only" → yes: the analysis request will contain the URL + our
  product's pedagogical purpose, plus Anthropic's web_fetch tool (capped at ~20
  fetches) so Claude itself chooses and reads the files — Anthropic's servers do
  the fetching; our request body carries no repo content. This actually
  improves on my seed heuristics: the model picks what to read.
  - Frontend/backend validation → mostly built already: client rejects
  non-GitHub URLs with the "not supported" message; the server confirms the repo
  is public + real via one GitHub metadata call
  (404/private/rate-limit/too-large each map to a friendly message) and pins the
  commit SHA.
  - "Don't re-learn continuously" → already engineered: the returned knowledge
  structure (RepositoryModel + evidence — your "tree of knowledge") is persisted
  keyed by (repo, commit SHA) and shared across users; every later
  lesson/assessment sends only the compact model + the one relevant excerpt. The
  repo is learned once per commit.
  - User level/requirements in the analysis → the prompt includes our product
  purpose and asks for per-concept goal applicability
  (understand/use/architecture/contribute), which is how user requirements scope
  the curriculum. I'd keep the analysis itself user-agnostic on purpose —
  that's what makes it cacheable across learners; personalization happens in the
  lesson loop.
  - Effort: moderate, not substantial — the ingestion/validation/caching work
  survives; what changes is the analyze route's internals (agentic loop + a
  strict "submit" tool instead of one big prompt). If the agentic loop
  misbehaves, the seed-push path I already built is the fallback. No need to
  hold off.

⏺ Update(docs/DECISIONS.md)
  ⎿  Added 22 lines
      183  
      184  ---
      185  
      186 +## 014 — Agentic repository analysis via web_fetch (2026-09-03
          +)                                                             
      187 +                                                              
      188 +**Decision:** Live analysis sends only the repository URL and 
          +the product's                                                 
      189 +purpose; Claude reads the repository itself through Anthropic'
          +s web_fetch tool                                              
      190 +(capped at 20 fetches) and returns the curriculum through a st
          +rict tool call.                                               
      191 +Our server still validates deterministically first (github.com
          + only, public,                                                
      192 +real, size-capped, commit SHA pinned) and sanitizes the result
          + (dedup ids,                                                  
      193 +drop dangling prerequisites) before it becomes state.         
      194 +                                                              
      195 +**Why:** The model chooses which files to read — better than f
          +ixed seed                                                     
      196 +heuristics — and the request carries no repository content. Pr
          +oduct owner                                                   
      197 +preference, and a stronger technical story.                   
      198 +                                                              
      199 +**Alternatives:** Server-side seed-file push (built first, kep
          +t as reference);                                              
      200 +custom read-only repo tools (list_directory/read_file) execute

⏺ Both phases are now at their gates — here's where everything stands.

  Phase 4 (persistence) — complete and verified in production, awaiting your 
  manual sign-off:
  - CI green, deployed, migrations ran at boot.
  - Verified live with two separate cookie identities: each "browser" gets its
  own seeded journey, a saved step survives re-fetch with learner state intact,
  and users are fully isolated.
  - Your manual test (~3 min): open https://understory.chat in your normal
  browser, run one demo lesson, then open the site in a different browser (or
  after clearing a private window) — you should see a fresh identity with only
  the fastapi seed. Back in your original browser: your progress is still there,
  now served from Postgres. The killer test vs. before: your journey now
  survives clearing localStorage (reload after clearing site data — the sidebar
  repopulates from the server).

  Phase 5 (live repos, agentic per your design) — built, all checks green, 
  waiting on PR #1:
  https://github.com/tomergafner/understory/pull/1 — not merged, per the
  always-submittable rule, until you sign off on Phase 4.

  What it does, exactly as you specified: the analyze request carries only the 
  URL and our product's purpose — Claude reads the repository itself via
  Anthropic's web_fetch tool (capped at 20 fetches, so it chooses ~a dozen files
  to actually read) and hands back the knowledge structure through a strict
  schema-validated tool call. Before any of that, our server deterministically
  confirms the URL is github.com and the repo is public/real/size-capped
  (friendly messages for private, not-found, too-large, rate-limited,
  non-GitHub); after it, deterministic sanitation runs before anything becomes
  state. The result — your "tree of knowledge" — is stored once per repo+commit
  and reused for every learner and every future lesson; no continuous
  re-learning. Onboarding unlocks all four goals for live repos, and the same
  adaptive loop teaches the analyzed repo.

  Suggested order from here:
  1. You manually test Phase 4 (production, above).
  2. On your sign-off, I merge PR #1 → CI deploys → I verify a real end-to-end
  analysis in production (I'll pick a mid-size repo, confirm the curriculum
  quality, timing, and caching) — then Phase 5 gets its own manual test from
  you: paste a repo you know well and judge whether the curriculum rings true.
  That's the best possible quality check.

  Both phase rituals are otherwise complete (checks, docs, decisions log updated
  — including 014 recording the agentic pivot and why). Take your time with the
  testing.

    analyzer copes with a large, GUI-heavy, embedded-adjacent codebase

  Repo: redis/redis

  What it is: TypeScript/React whiteboard app
  Why it's a good test: Frontend product with real architecture (scene graph,
    collaboration); very different reading style from the others

  Expectations while testing: analysis is 1–3 minutes on first visit per repo
  (with honest progress copy), instant afterward (cached by commit); on the very
  large ones (qgroundcontrol, numpy) Claude only reads ~a dozen files within
  its fetch budget, so judge whether it picked the right files — that's the
  interesting quality question. Choosing different goals on the same repo should
  yield visibly different scope.

  The goal-selection fix — you were right, now shipped

  The lock was a Phase 1 leftover (the scripted demo only had an architecture
  path). Fixed: all four goals are now selectable for every journey, the fixture
  curriculum is retagged so understand/use paths exist for the demo, and your
  chosen goal was already flowing into every Claude request (it scopes which
  concepts are in-scope, drives the coverage denominator, and appears in each
  lesson/assess/review prompt as "Learner goal: X" with per-concept
  in-scope/out-of-scope markings). The onboarding copy now says so: "Your goal 
  decides which concepts are in scope and how deep the path goes."

  One production bug found and fixed during verification

  My live test of the analyzer hit HTTP 524 — Cloudflare enforces a hard
  ~100-second proxy timeout, and analysis takes longer. Rearchitected as an
  async job: the POST starts the analysis in the background and returns 202 
  analyzing; the client re-POSTs every 5 seconds until done. The in-flight
  registry doubles as the duplicate-analysis lock CLAUDE.md wanted (no Redis
  needed). All 42 tests, lint, typecheck green; CI is deploying it now.

  Once the deploy lands, I'll verify the full analyze→lesson flow in production
  and hand you the keys for manual testing with the list above.

      112 +export function mergeDeepModel(                               
      113 +  journey: { model?: RepositoryModel; learner: { conceptStatus
          +: Record<string, string> } },                                 
      114 +  deep: RepositoryModel,                                      
      115 +): RepositoryModel {                                          
      116 +  const starter = journey.model;                              
      117 +  if (!starter) return { ...deep, partial: undefined };       
      118 +  const deepIds = new Set(deep.concepts.map((c) => c.id));    
      119 +  const touchedMissing = starter.concepts.filter(             
      120 +    (c) =>                                                    
      121 +      (journey.learner.conceptStatus[c.id] ?? "untaught") !== 
          +"untaught" &&                                                 
      122 +      !deepIds.has(c.id),                                     
      123 +  );                                                          
      124 +  return {                                                    
      125 +    ...deep,                                                  
      126 +    concepts: [...deep.concepts, ...touchedMissing],          
      127 +    partial: undefined,                                       
      128 +  };                                                          
      129 +}

⏺ The two-stage redesign is in the pipeline (local e2e gates the commit, then CI
  → deploy). What you'll experience once it lands:

  New flow: paste URL → ~20 seconds ("Getting a first read… your learning path
  starts in about twenty seconds") → onboarding opens with a 6–8 concept starter
  curriculum built from the repo's README → you're learning immediately.
  Meanwhile the deep agentic pass (Claude actually reading the code) runs in the
  background, seeded with the starter's concept ids so they stay stable. The
  journey header shows a quiet pulsing "deep analysis running…" note; when it
  finishes, your curriculum upgrades in place — new concepts appear, evidence
  excerpts start showing up in lessons, and the coverage denominator grows
  visibly (consistent with the product's "path grew" philosophy). If the deep
  pass ever fails, you keep learning on the starter without interruption.

  On your error: server logs showed no analyzer failures — the "Something went
  wrong reaching the analyzer" message is the client's catch-all for a non-JSON
  reply, which matches testing during the deploy churn from the earlier pipeline
  race (now prevented by the CI concurrency guard), or a browser tab still
  running the pre-fix bundle. Either way the new architecture eliminates the
  class of problem: the only long-running call left is a background job.

  I'll verify the full two-stage flow in production the moment this deploy
  completes, then hand you the five-repo test list for real.

⏺ The audit's top prediction just proved itself live: my probes alone exhausted
  GitHub's unauthenticated rate limit — production now returns rate_limited for
  every new analysis, and the logs also confirm the server restarted and
  silently re-ran the quick analysis twice (memory wiped, commander.js's deep
  pass lost). Here is the full synthesis.

  ---
  Demo-Readiness Audit — 48 confirmed findings (0 refuted), 50 verification 
  agents

  Ground truth first: 42 unit/route tests, typecheck, lint, build, and 3 e2e
  tests all pass; stage-1 analysis verified live (13.7s, sensible starter
  curriculum); a lesson against a partial model verified live (14.6s, real
  content). The problems below are what the green checks don't cover.

  🔴 Demo-breakers — will fail in front of an evaluator

  - Live-repo journeys lose their model on save; reload white-screens the entire
  app (4 finders independently, verified c=0.97). The journeys.model column
  exists but journeyToRow/assembleJourney/upsertJourney never touch it. Learn
  any live repo → reload → server copy (modelless) overwrites localStorage →
  modelForJourney throws Unknown fixture repo: gh:... → the sidebar renders on
  every route and there's no error.tsx → the whole app is bricked for that 
  cookie identity, permanently. This is the exact leave-and-return flow the
  take-home centers on. Mitigation: ~5-line mapping fix + defensive null-return 
  in modelForJourney + a minimal error.tsx; extend the round-trip test (it never
  asserted model).
  - GitHub rate-limit death spiral — just reproduced in production. Every 10s
  poll makes 2 unauthenticated GitHub calls (60/hr shared per server IP); a
  single deep analysis + polling kills the budget in minutes, after which polls
  fail silently ("deep analysis running…" forever) and every new repo paste 
  fails. GITHUB_TOKEN is not set on Railway and absent from the README env
  table. Mitigation: set GITHUB_TOKEN (→5,000/hr) today; make polls skip 
  resolveRepoHead when the commit is already known (pass repoId+sha, cache heads
  briefly).
  - Goal dead-end at 0%. Pick a goal the starter curriculum barely tags (e.g.
  "contribute" on a README-only starter) → nextUntaughtConcept returns null →
  instant "Path complete" at 0% with a review that 404s. Mitigation: onboarding 
  shows/disables goals with zero in-scope concepts, or falls back to 
  all-concepts scope when a goal's slice is empty.
  - Live repos display demo copy: the analysis animation says "Demo uses a 
  bundled analysis — no waiting" with fake stages — while a real analysis runs.
  Dishonest at the worst moment. (3 finders.) Mitigation: conditional copy + 
  reuse the real progress language.
  - Assignment deliverables not yet packaged (written rationale, ~5-min video,
  transcript export) — planned for Phase 7, but flagging: nothing else matters
  if these are missing at submission. (Verifier downgraded to schedule-risk, 
  correctly.)

  🟠 Serious degradations — wrong or embarrassing, not fatal

  - Goal chosen at onboarding is silently discarded — onConflictDoUpdate omits
  goal/goalLabel; resume teaches/measures the wrong goal. Fix: add two fields to
  the update set.
  - Deep-merge edge: the merged model can drop the recommended-next (untaught)
  concept → raw kebab-case id in the Continue button and a server prompt
  containing literally undefined. undefined. Fix: merge keeps the 
  recommendedNext concept; lesson route rejects unknown conceptIds.
  - Coverage visibly drops unexplained at merge (e.g. "25% → 9%" mid-feedback)
  and the upgrade is silent on success, dishonest on failure (permanent "deep
  analysis running…", plus every page load relaunches the expensive deep job
  after a failure). Fix: one adaptation-style note when the merge lands; persist
  deep-failure into the journey.
  - Restart race: demo restart fires DELETE and PUT without ordering — the
  DELETE can land last and erase the restarted journey server-side. Fix: drop 
  the DELETE (the createdAt-wipe already handles restart) .
  - Reload before the first completed step replays the fake analysis + 
  onboarding, discarding typed answers ("fresh" is inferred from empty
  conceptStatus). Fix: explicit onboarded flag on the journey.
  - Fallback grading lies on live repos: on a model error, free-form answers
  with no expectedKeywords grade any non-empty text as correct. Fix: for live 
  repos, surface retry instead of fake-grading.
  - Broad-review mastery misattribution: question→concept mapping by array
  index; the wrong concept can take the mastery hit. Fix: carry conceptId per 
  question in the plan schema.
  - "Multiple choice only" not enforced on model lessons; nextUntaughtConcept
  ignores prerequisites (teaching-order jumps on live repos). Fixes: server-side
  style filter; prereq-aware selection.
  - Assess wait UX: during the 10–20s grade, the quiz stays editable and
  mid-grade edits are silently discarded; only the button text changes. Fix: 
  disable inputs while grading.
  - Unauthenticated cost amplifier: /api/analyze starts Fable jobs with no
  throttle or concurrency cap — risky on a public URL during evaluation. Fix: 
  tiny in-memory per-IP throttle + max concurrent deep jobs.
  - README materially wrong (still describes the Phase 1 fixture prototype; env
  table missing DATABASE_URL/GITHUB_TOKEN); docs lag the two-stage architecture;
  code cites a DECISIONS entry that doesn't exist.
  - A11y/responsive: no focus management across full-screen view swaps, sr-only
  radio pills lack visible focus, zero responsive breakpoints (a phone glance is
  broken), first load is a blank div with no skeleton.

  🟡 Test debt (what would have caught the above)

  - Zero tests: analysis-jobs state machine, mergeDeepModel, store
  dual-write/fallback (HTTP-error PUTs are silently dropped — only network
  failures flip to local mode), journeys API + identity.
  - e2e never exercises: live-repo flow, reviews, restart, partial→deep upgrade
  — and CI's keyless run doesn't cover the keyed path evaluators actually hit.
  Mitigation: jobs/merge/store unit tests (cheap, high yield) + one scripted 
  prod smoke checklist for the recorded demo.

  Recommended fix order

  P0 (before anyone touches live repos again, ~1–2h): model persistence + error
  boundary → GITHUB_TOKEN + poll de-duplication → goal persistence → live-repo
  analysis copy → goal dead-end guard → restart race.
  P1 (before the recorded demo): merge-note + deep-failure persistence,
  assess-lock, fallback-grading honesty, review attribution, README refresh.
  P2 (Phase 6 polish): a11y/focus/responsive/skeleton, style enforcement,
  prereq-aware ordering, throttle, test debt.

  Full details (file:line evidence, per-finding verifier reasoning) are
  preserved in the audit output. Say the word and I'll execute P0 immediately.

  client-contract gap). My own monitoring traffic helped burn the quota, which
  is precisely what an evaluator's open browser tab would do at 2 calls per
  10-second poll.

  So two of the P0 items now have live production evidence, not just static

      173 +// Head resolution is cached briefly: the client polls /api/an
          +alyze every 10s                                               
      174 +// while a deep analysis runs, and each uncached resolve costs
          + 2 GitHub API                                                 
      175 +// calls against a 60/hr unauthenticated budget (verified exha
          +usted in prod).                                               
      176 +const headCache = new Map<string, { head: RepoHead; at: number
          + }>();                                                        
      177 +const HEAD_TTL_MS = 10 * 60_000;                              
      178 +                                                              
      179  export async function resolveRepoHead(urlInput: string): Promi
           se<RepoHead> {
      180    const { owner, repo, repoId } = parseGitHubUrl(urlInput);
      181 +                                                              
      182 +  const cached = headCache.get(repoId);                       
      183 +  if (cached && Date.now() - cached.at < HEAD_TTL_MS) return c
          +ached.head;                                                   
      184 +                                                              
      185 +  const head = await resolveRepoHeadUncached(owner, repo, repo
          +Id);                                                          
      186 +  headCache.set(repoId, { head, at: Date.now() });            
      187 +  return head;                                                
      188 +}                                                             
      189 +                                                              
      190 +async function resolveRepoHeadUncached(                       
      191 +  owner: string,                                              
      192 +  repo: string,                                               
      193 +  repoId: string,                                             
      194 +): Promise<RepoHead> {

      197      description: string | null;

⏺ P0 verified in production; P1 shipped and in the pipeline.

  P0 verification results (live):
  - White-screen fix confirmed — a live journey PUT with its model came back
  from the server intact: hasModel: true, 7 concepts, partial flag preserved,
  and the goal ("use") persisted too. The single worst bug from the audit is
  dead.
  - Rate-limit fix confirmed — analysis works again: chalk re-analyzed in 17s
  (8-concept starter, deepFailed: false) with your GITHUB_TOKEN active and the
  10-minute head cache eliminating the poll drain. No rate_limited responses.
  - Deep-analysis completion — monitor armed; this is the last lifecycle piece
  never yet observed end-to-end (previous attempts died to restarts/rate limits
  — both causes now fixed).
  - Copy fix, goal dead-end guard, and restart-race removal are client-side and
  rode the same deploy.

  P1 shipped (46 → 47 tests green, typecheck, lint, e2e; CI running now):
  - Deep-merge lands with a visible amber note ("deep analysis complete — N
  concepts joined your path, coverage recalculated"); deep failure is persisted
  into the journey — no more perpetual "running…" indicator or
  relaunch-on-every-reload, header switches to "learning from the starter
  curriculum".
  - The merge now always retains the recommended-next concept — Continue can
  never point at a vanished concept, and the lesson route independently rejects
  unknown concept ids (no more undefined reaching prompts).
  - Quiz inputs lock during the 10–20s grading call (edits were silently
  discarded before).
  - Live repos fail visibly on grading errors with answers preserved, instead of
  fake keyword-grading.
  - "Multiple choice only" is enforced server-side; review questions now carry
  their conceptId so mastery lands on the right concept.
  - README rewritten to describe the actual product; 4 new mergeDeepModel
  regression tests.

  Two watchers pending: the chalk deep-analysis completion and P1's CI deploy.
  When both land I'll confirm the full partial → deep → cached lifecycle in
  production — at that point the five-repo manual test is genuinely ready for
  you.

⏺ Monitor event: "quiet-window: chalk deep analysis lifecycle"

⏺ Noted — the monitor expired with chalk still partial after 15 minutes. Two
  possible readings: the deep agentic pass legitimately takes that long (Fable
  at medium effort with up to 20 web fetches and a multi-turn loop — plausible),
  or it's dying silently with container restarts (the push mid-window muddies
  the signal). The good news: the server logs each model turn, so once this
  deploy lands I can distinguish the two directly — progressing analyze turn
  entries in the logs means it's slow-but-alive, absence means it's dying.

  Worth saying: even if deep analysis takes 15–20 minutes on some repos, the
  two-stage UX absorbs it — the learner is already deep in starter-curriculum
  lessons, and the merge simply arrives later. The demo risk isn't the latency;
  it's only ever the silent-death case, which the logs will now settle.
  Diagnosis continues the moment CI reports.

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The streaming fix is shipped and the pipeline chain will automatically kick
  off a fresh chalk analysis the moment the deploy goes green — then this
  becomes the true quiet window with the actual bug fixed.

  To recap the root cause for the record: every "deep analysis never finishes"
  symptom across the whole session traces to one line — the agentic turn used a
  non-streaming messages.create, and a turn that spends many minutes in the
  server-side web_fetch loop exceeds the SDK's ~10-minute non-streaming timeout,
  then dies silently through retries and deploys. Streaming with finalMessage()
  is the documented cure, and per-turn progress logs mean we'll see the loop
  advancing this time ({"at":"analyze","turn":N}).

  When the watcher fires I'll monitor the analysis to completion, verify the
  merge + Postgres persistence + evidence-grounded lessons, and finally clear
  you for the five-repo test.
